#2868. Christian McCaffrey headlines 9 in-season trades this decade that were big hits, with 9 others that were busts

The Seattle Seahawks would not have won a Super Bowl without some smart in-season trades for veterans on both sides of the ball.

The 2025 Seahawks are represented well on the list of best in-season trades since 2020. The trade deadline is Tuesday, and there has already been a lot of action. Expect plenty more deals before next week, with teams hoping to acquire that one player who can push them into a Super Bowl.

Here are the nine best and worst in-season trades in the NFL since the 2020 season:

This trade was so good for the 49ers, it's hard to remember McCaffrey as a member of the Panthers. The 49ers paid up for McCaffrey, sending draft picks in the second, third, fourth and fifth rounds for a back who at that point was dynamic but had played just 10 games the previous two seasons combined due to injuries. It turned out to be a bargain for the 49ers.

49ers running back Christian McCaffrey has been one of the NFL's best players since he was traded to San Francisco. (AP Photo/Brynn Anderson)

Here's what McCaffrey has done since the trade: 6,347 yards from scrimmage and 56 total touchdowns in 4 1/2 seasons, with NFL Offensive Player of the Year and NFL Comeback Player of the Year awards. McCaffrey has cemented himself as a future Hall of Famer and one of the best running backs ever. San Francisco made one Super Bowl with McCaffrey and is a contender to do it again this season.

The Panthers used those picks to help them get Bryce Young in a big trade, and also a trade up to get edge rusher D.J. Johnson in the third round. Johnson had a half of a sack before he was out of the NFL. It was a heist for San Francisco.

Jones had a weird 2024. He was traded from the Rams to the Titans right before the season for a late-round draft pick swap. The Titans had a terrible season and then unloaded Jones to the Seahawks for linebacker Jerome Baker and a fourth-round pick. All Jones has done since then is become one of the NFL's best off-ball linebackers. He has 301 tackles, seven interceptions and four sacks in 33 regular-season games for Seattle. He's a remarkable fit for Mike Macdonald's defense. He was a key component to the Seahawks' Super Bowl championship team. Baker played five games for the Titans and is out of football this season, and the pick became tight end Gunnar Helm, who has 517 career yards over two seasons.

Smith hasn't brought a championship to the Ravens but he has been a stellar performer. In his first three full seasons with Baltimore he was a Pro Bowler each time. The Bears got a second-round pick, used on defensive tackle Gervon Dexter Sr. (before this season Chicago traded Dexter to the Falcons for cornerback Clark Phillips III and a 2027 fifth-round draft pick, and since then Dexter signed a four-year, $78 million extension), a fifth-round pick and linebacker A.J. Klein, who didn't record a stat in two games for Chicago.

Miller played 12 games for the Rams, including playoffs. He had a massive impact in that short time. Miller had five sacks in the regular season and four more in the playoffs, which led all players that postseason. He had two sacks in a Super Bowl win over the Bengals. The Rams might not have won that championship without Miller. The Broncos did well too. They got a second- and third-round pick for Miller. The second-round pick became defensive end Nik Bonitto, one of the league's best pass rushers. A true win-win deal.

The Seahawks sent a second- and fifth-round pick to the Giants for Williams, which was a steep price for a 29-year-old defensive lineman. Williams made Pro Bowls in 2024 and 2025 and has been a leader and perfect fit for one of the NFL's best defenses. Last season, the Seahawks won a Super Bowl with Williams being a huge part of it. The price wasn't so steep after all.

Too soon? Maybe. But it was undeniable that the Bears' defense looked completely different with Simmons in the middle in his debut. The price was heavy, with two first-round picks going to the Titans. But we'll project a bit and say Chicago will be happy to have Simmons, one of the top interior defensive players in the NFL.

Funny enough, had the Commanders just used the 40th overall draft pick that the Bears sent them for Sweat, the trade would have looked better. That pick was defensive back Cooper DeJean, who has become a good player … for the Eagles. The Commanders traded down and took cornerback Mike Sainristil and tight end Ben Sinnott. Neither have worked out. Sweat has 24.5 sacks in 48 games for the Bears and with Jeffery Simmons coming aboard this season, is a key part of a good defensive line for a Super Bowl contender.

Shaheed has just 351 receiving yards in 16 regular-season games for the Seahawks, since Seattle sent fourth- and fifth-round picks to New Orleans for the big-play receiver. Why is this a positive trade? Because Shaheed made perhaps the biggest play of the Seahawks' Super Bowl run, with a 58-yard punt return with about eight minutes left with Seattle trailing the Rams 30-14 in a Week 16 game. That sparked a huge comeback that led to a Seahawks overtime win, and it was a springboard to a championship. Shaheed also started the playoff run with a 95-yard kickoff return for a touchdown on the opening play of a divisional-round rout of the 49ers. Those big returns were well worth the cost.

Toney's Chiefs career didn't end great, so you might remember the drops, penalties and controversies and think it was a bad trade. And that's reasonable. But Toney made two enormous plays in a Super Bowl win over the Eagles. Kansas City trailed 27-21 in the fourth quarter when Toney scored a 5-yard touchdown and then had a 65-yard punt return to set up another short touchdown. The Chiefs won 38-35 on a field goal as time expired. Toney might have been a headache overall, but without sending those third- and sixth-round picks to the Giants, do the Chiefs win Super Bowl LVII?

In the moment, this trade didn't seem so bad. The Colts were 7-2 when they traded for Gardner, one of the best cornerbacks in the NFL. The price was huge, sending two first-round draft picks and receiver Adonai Mitchell to New York. But it looked like the Colts were getting an impact player for a playoff push. Since then the Colts are 3-13 and have fired general manager Chris Ballard. Mitchell has dealt with injuries but is coming off a 142-yard game. The Colts' collapse isn't all Gardner's fault, but he has been injured and has not done much for Indianapolis. Meanwhile, the Colts continue to trade players, and their first-round pick next year that will go to the Jets could be in the top five. Meanwhile, it's arguable that Mitchell's value has caught up to Gardner, or at least made it close for a player whom Indianapolis included as a rage throw-in after Mitchell dropped the ball before scoring a touchdown against the Chargers. Given the cost and return on investment, it's probably the most damaging trade on this list.

Even in the moment it seemed questionable that the Bears would send what would end up being a high second-round pick to the Steelers for Claypool, who was inconsistent on the field. It ended up being one of the worst trades in the NFL over many years. For the rest of Claypool's career he had 22 catches for 117 yards and one touchdown. Claypool was out of the NFL at age 26. Meanwhile, the pick sent to the Steelers ended up being 32nd overall and was used on Joey Porter Jr., who became one of the league's best young corners (and was traded to the Cowboys for a second- and sixth-round pick this season).

When the Jets traded for Adams, everyone wondered what they were thinking. The trade was made the day after the Jets fell to 2-4. They had already fired head coach Robert Saleh. They still sent a third-round pick to the Raiders for Adams. He played well, with 854 yards, but the Jets predictably went 5-12 and the team released Adams the next offseason due to salary cap reasons. The Raiders didn't exactly put that pick they got to good use. They traded it to Seattle for Geno Smith. And the Seahawks burned that on quarterback Jalen Milroe, who has had no impact.

Washington traded third-, fourth- and sixth-round picks to the Saints for Lattimore and a fifth-round pick. At the time Lattimore was considered a top cornerback. But the Commanders quickly found that Lattimore was in decline. He did not play well in 11 games with the team, tore his ACL during the 2025 season, was released for salary cap reasons this past offseason and remains unsigned.

Part of the error of this trade was the Eagles seemingly miscalculating the market on Phillips, who was to be a 2026 free agent. The Eagles traded a third-round pick to the Dolphins for Phillips. He played eight games for Philadelphia and had two sacks for a team that faded hard at the end of the season and did not win a playoff game. Phillips then left the Eagles in free agency, signing a four-year, $120 million deal in Carolina. The Eagles didn't expect that, and reacted by trading two third-round picks to the Vikings for Jonathan Greenard, a 29-year-old edge rusher who has two tackles and no sacks this season. Greenard signed a four-year, $98 million extension after the trade. So it can be argued the final cost of the Phillips deal was three third-round picks and $98 million for a total of two sacks from a pair of players.

The Bills thought they were getting a key weapon for Josh Allen when they traded a third- and seventh-round pick to the Browns for Cooper and a sixth. Cooper was almost immediately hurt with a wrist injury and made practically no impact. Cooper had 297 yards and two touchdowns with the Bills, Buffalo lost the AFC championship game to the Chiefs (Cooper had 33 yards in that game) and Cooper never played another down in the NFL after that season. The third-round pick was used by the Browns on quarterback Dillon Gabriel, who hasn't worked out either.

The Cowboys' trade for Mingo was surprising because they sent a fourth-round pick to Carolina for a player who had done very little in the NFL. And Mingo has done even less with the Cowboys. Since that trade two years ago he has nine catches for 101 yards and no touchdowns. The Cowboys might have thought they were outsmarting the rest of the NFL with that deal, but it turns out everyone else was correct in thinking Dallas had just made a mistake.

At the time the Jaguars seemed to be buying low on Ridley, who was serving a year-long suspension for betting on NFL games. The trade compensation was conditional and ultimately Jacksonville sent the 79th overall pick of the 2024 draft to the Falcons for Ridley. What the Jaguars got for that was a modest 1,016 yards in one 9-8 season with Ridley, who left for Tennessee as a free agent.

It might be harsh to put the Chubb deal on this list. He was productive, with 22 sacks in 41 games. But the Dolphins swung big on a deal to improve their chances of a deep playoff run, and that didn't happen. The Dolphins sent a first-round pick along with a fourth and running back Chase Edmonds to Denver for Chubb and a fifth. The Dolphins didn't win a division title or playoff game after the trade, tore the roster down this past offseason and Chubb's $10.97 dead cap hit is part of the overwhelming overall dead cap hit of more than $188 million for the 2026 Dolphins.

→ Baca artikel asli

#2867. Can the Suns fix this from within?

Not great. Some people would pick less euphemistic words, but I'm trying to be nice. I'll admit I didn't envision this happening. I never expected the Suns to be world beaters, but I also didn't expect them to be a punching bag as they stumbled out of the gate.

The most glaring deficiency with the Suns through the first six games is defense. The Suns are second to last in points per game allowed, behind only the Chicago Bulls, at 124.3 points per game. After a bleak 1-4 start, they surrendered a season-high 132 points last night when they knew this was a critical match to right the ship. The Suns are dead last in Defensive Rating, allowing 125.6 points per 100 possessions.

The Suns need to find a lineup that can get stops. That should be the main priority in practices. Switch people around and try to get something to click. Switch people around in the games if they aren't giving maximum effort. I'm fine with anybody sitting down and watching what effort looks like.

Sometimes it appears the Suns struggle with fundamentals like switching or fighting over screens. Effort, strategy, and execution. One hand needs to know what the other is doing.

Someone I went to the game with last night, who isn't a basketball aficionado, said it just seemed like Portland was scoring so fast and easily. I guess you don't have to be a basketball genius to see the Suns suck at defense right now. She said the Suns also missed a lot of threes. The Suns could have been better. They shot 10-of-36 from deep for a 27.8% clip. Maybe if another two or three of those went in when the Suns were trying to close the gap, things could have turned out a little differently.

I told her that, the way I look at it, three-point shooting is mostly something that improves with practice. It's not like defense, where if a player gives maximum effort, their performance can improve in real time. Effort doesn't affect shooting in the same way it does defense. A lot of defense is effort, and the Suns aren't meeting the standard they set last season.

I have always considered defensive rebounding as part of defense. That word in front of rebounding is the hint. A defensive rebound ends an offensive possession, and ending the opponent's possession is important. Portland snatched 16 offensive rebounds against the Suns, who are relinquishing the third-most offensive boards in the league at 14.5 per game. Boxing out might help. I hope they realize that because they are not doing it.

The Suns need to rebound by committee. Khaman Maluach is leading the team in rebounds per 36 minutes with 11.2, but other holes in his game prevent him from being a true anchor at this early stage.

Last season, Mark Williams was second on the team in rebounds per 36 minutes, and Jordan Goodwin was sixth. Goodwin was also second in defensive rating. Getting these guys back could definitely have a positive effect, but for the Suns to turn this around, they will need to ramp up effort. This isn't just a scheme issue.

Where is the continuity, chemistry, and…. joy?

The Suns have 10 players averaging over 10 minutes a game this season. Of those 10, eight were teammates on the Suns last season. Only Miles Bridges and Luke Kennard are being incorporated into whatever system the Suns are attempting to run. This year the Suns don't look like they dislike each other, but they don't have the same energy.

One possible cause of the perceived chemistry issues is that Jalen Green is being asked to play a different role, more as a distributor. Unfortunately, through six games, he is averaging just 3.3 assists, which is exactly his career average. The Suns are now 18-20 in regular-season games when Green plays. 18-24, counting the bloodbath at the hands of the Oklahoma City Thunder. The Suns might be trying to shift from Point Book to Point Green, but right now it looks like an experiment with less-than-impressive results.

Sometimes I think it might just be easier for the Suns to get a real point guard. Then other players would be freed up to exploit their strengths. This happened when Chris Paul played with Devin Booker, and they had a decent run, but that isn't this roster.

Ott needs to determine who is best equipped for this role, since there isn't a perfect fit. If it is Green, then Jalen needs to figure this out fast. The clock is ticking. If it isn't Green, then Ott needs to figure this out fast. The clock is ticking.

I may have softened a little in the midsection as I've gotten older, but the Suns' underbelly is softer than an armadillo's. The problem is the Suns don't have a protective shell. They are just kind of soft overall right now.

Maluach looks like the game is entirely too fast for him at this stage. By the time he sees where he needs to be, the play has already evolved past the point for him to get involved. Maluach has obvious talent, but his positioning needs a lot of work and… he has hands of stone. Oso Ighodaro is averaging 4.3 assists per game and has demonstrated the ability as a playmaker to find cutting teammates for easy baskets, but he lacks the size of a true big man and will be at a disadvantage almost nightly.

The Suns need to rebound as a team. Last season Mark Williams was second on the team in rebounds per 36 minutes, and Jordan Goodwin was second. Goodwin was also second in defensive rating. Getting these guys back could definitely have an effect, but for the Suns to turn this around, they will need to ramp up effort. This isn't just a scheme issue.

Can the Suns fix this from within? I don't know. The optics have not been encouraging.

There are things that we should at least hope to see.

1. Find a lineup that clicks defensively. Practice overall schemes and implement changes specific to the opponent. Players need to give maximum effort or keep the bench warm for someone who will. Maybe Jordan Goodwin's return will help, but this needs to happen immediately. This should be attainable. Since the team is essentially worst in the league they could still be "terrible", as Dillon Brooks described… and improve.

2. Figure out the rotations and how this offense actually needs to flow. The numbers here aren't as pitiful as the defensive side, but the Suns are 26th in the league in pace, averaging only 97.3 points per 100 possessions. They are slow and constantly look disjointed. I can watch a losing team play basketball, but bad, boring basketball is a big ask.

3. Get Mark Williams and Jordan Goodwin back. The Suns' injuries are exposing their weaknesses. Defense and rebounding are the biggest culprits for this start, and those are the main things those two bring to the table. Right now nobody is eating at that table. The Suns are better equipped to suffer an injury at other positions. The Green/Brooks/Booker combo should actually give good insurance with some overlapping skills. Those two wouldn't make this team 5-1 right now, but anything would help.

4. Find a way for Booker to be a better version of himself. If Booker just plays like a good player, and not an elite All-NBA type of player, this team probably won't make the playoffs. Steve Nash won MVP in Phoenix at 30, but Booker is not passing the eye test at the same age.

5. Try harder. Please make the fans think the players want to win as bad as the fans do.

6. Prayer. If any of you are religious or spiritual, it couldn't hurt.

7. Jordan Ott's honeymoon is over. Time for the part of marriage that takes a lot of work. Hopefully Ott and the players are ready to commit. Hopefully Ott is up to the challenge. He has an imperfect roster and he has to get players in their best roles to succeed…. even if he hurts someone's feelings.

If these things don't happen, it might be a long season… or potentially short for the people who can't stomach it.

#2866. Patriots Have Good and Bad News on Key Players Before Packers Game

FOXBOROUGH, MASSACHUSETTS - OCTOBER 11: Hunter Henry #85 of the New England Patriots catches a touchdown pass against the Las Vegas Raiders during the second quarter at Gillette Stadium on October 11, 2026 in Foxborough, Massachusetts. (Photo by Edward Monigan IV/Getty Images) Getty

CHICAGO, ILLINOIS - OCTOBER 22: Christian Barmore #90 of the New England Patriots walks off the field after a play against the Chicago Bears during the fourth quarter of the game at Soldier Field on October 22, 2026 in Chicago, Illinois. (Photo by Patrick McDermott/Getty Images) Getty

The New England Patriots received some encouraging news regarding one of their key defensive players Thursday, but concerns are growing about the availability of a veteran offensive weapon.

Defensive tackle Christian Barmore returned to practice Thursday, Nov. 5, after missing Wednesday's session with an illness.

However, tight end Hunter Henry remained sidelined for a second consecutive day as he continues recovering from a foot injury sustained against the Miami Dolphins.

The contrasting occurences come as the Patriots prepare for a highly anticipated Week 9 matchup with the Green Bay Packers on Sunday, Nov. 8.

Both teams enter the contest with 5-3 records.

GettyFOXBOROUGH, MASSACHUSETTS – OCTOBER 11: Hunter Henry #85 of the New England Patriots catches a touchdown pass against the Las Vegas Raiders during the second quarter at Gillette Stadium on October 11, 2026 in Foxborough, Massachusetts. (Photo by Edward Monigan IV/Getty Images)

Henry's availability against Green Bay is becoming a growing concern after the veteran tight end missed another practice Thursday.

ESPN's Mike Reiss reported the misfortune on X Thursday afternoon, noting that Henry was absent after being limited to just eight offensive snaps against Miami.

Henry suffered the foot injury during New England's 24-19 victory over the Dolphins on Sunday, Nov. 1, and was unable to return to the contest.

The Patriots subsequently listed the 31-year-old as a nonparticipant on Wednesday's official injury report.

Henry has established himself as a dependable target for quarterback Drake Maye, making his potential absence particularly concerning as New England deals with injuries across its receiving corps.

Wide receiver A.J. Brown also missed Wednesday's practice while dealing with ankle and knee issues.

If Henry cannot play Sunday, the Patriots could lean more heavily on tight ends Eli Raridon, Tanner Arkin and Cameron Latu.

Henry's status for the Packers matchup remains uncertain, with Friday's final injury report expected to provide additional clarity.

GettyCHICAGO, ILLINOIS – OCTOBER 22: Christian Barmore #90 of the New England Patriots walks off the field after a play against the Chicago Bears during the fourth quarter of the game at Soldier Field on October 22, 2026 in Chicago, Illinois. (Photo by Patrick McDermott/Getty Images)

While Henry's absence presents a setback, New England received more encouraging news on the defensive side of the ball.

Barmore returned to the practice field Thursday after missing Wednesday's session because of an illness.

WEEI reporter Justin Turpin shared video of Barmore on X, showing the defensive tackle stretching alongside head coach Mike Vrabel.

Barmore's return comes one day after Vrabel confirmed that the defender was dealing with an illness that would prevent him from participating in Wednesday's practice.

The 26-year-old played in New England's victory over Miami, contributing half a sack before missing the team's first practice of the week.

Although Barmore's official Thursday participation designation had not yet been released, his presence on the practice field represents a positive development for the Patriots.

New England will look to have its defensive front at full strength against Jordan Love and Green Bay's offense Sunday.

The Patriots will release their final injury designations Friday, providing a clearer picture of whether Henry and Barmore will be available.

#2865. A.J. Brown knee injury ‘raises some concerns’ about Patriots future

The New England Patriots trading first- and fourth-round draft picks to the Philadelphia Eagles for wide receiver A.J. Brown was a gamble.

On the one hand, he was a proven No. 1 wide receiver capable of adding a new level of playmaking ability to what was one of the best offenses in football in 2025. On the other hand, he was heading into his age-29 season off of his least productive season in four years.

Eight weeks into the season, the gamble has not paid off. Brown has appeared in just three games so far, catching nine passes for 101 yards. A bigger concern than his output, however, is his injury situation. Not only did he spend four games on injured reserve with a high ankle sprain, he also is currently dealing with a knee injury that has forced him to miss four straight practices and last Sunday's win in Miami.

While that knee injury has not prompted another move to IR, Brown's short-term availability has been impacted. And beyond that, there are questions about his long-term outlook as well as addressed by ESPN's Adam Schefter on Thursday morning.

"Let's go back to the offseason, when Philadelphia was shopping A.J. Brown and they were talking to teams," Schefter said. "One of the reasons that I believe there wasn't more interest was there were some teams that had some questions about his knee. In fact, when they were advanced discussions for a trade with the Los Angeles Rams that was at one point close to being completed … I think the Rams had some concerns about his knee. The concerns that other teams have had have now manifested themselves in New England this season.

"It flared up. … The knee held him out last week, and the knee is threatening to hold him out this week. It's a knee that I think raises some concerns as to how long he's going to keep playing here in New England, and producing at a high level."

Worries about Brown's knee are nothing new. Patriots executive vice president of player personnel Eliot Wolf himself spoke about them earlier this year.

"It's a factor, but we examined him," Wolf said back in June. "He's got a knee that has some wear and tear that we were aware of. Our training staff and medical staff signed off on it."

Regardless of his current availability issues, the Patriots moving on from Brown in the near future is not expected. His contract structure alone would likely prevent such a move from happening: if released next offseason, for example, New England would take on a dead cap charge of almost $26 million.

For the time being, though, Brown remains with the Patriots and part of their 53-man roster. How quickly he can return to the field will be seen, though; one line of thinking is that he might be kept out through the team's trip to Germany next week and into the Week 11 bye to get fully ready for the stretch run.

#2864. Top Democrats call to rein in AI buildout after midterms sweep: 'National data center moratorium now'

After a big win in the U.S. midterm elections on Tuesday, some Democrats are already signaling they want to take on the artificial build-out, specifically data centers.

In a post on X, Sen. Bernie Sanders, I-Vt., touted referendums across the U.S. on Tuesday where voters in localities approved restrictions on data centers. Sanders, who caucuses with the Democrats, said the message to political leaders is clear.

"National data center moratorium now," he wrote in the post. "Americans don't want Big Tech oligarchs wrecking the environment, gutting jobs and threatening humanity's future."

In another post on X, Rep. Yassamin Ansari, D-Ariz., wrote that pausing AI development needs to be a top priority for the large Democratic majority in the House on Day 1. Rep. Greg Casar, D-Texas, the chair of the Congressional Progressive Caucus, amplified the post and said, "Yes!"

According to the CNBC Voter Poll, 56% of voters said AI and data centers were a crucial or major factor in their decision at the ballot box. Of those voters, 65% said they cast a ballot for a Democratic candidate for Congress.

Stocks linked to the artificial intelligence build-out declined slightly on Wednesday in the wake of the election, but the selling intensified on Thursday as investors worried Democratic wins could have a real impact on data center construction. Chip stocks led the decline. The iShares Semiconductor ETF (SOXX) tumbled nearly 9% on Tuesday. Nvidia declined 8%, while Advanced Micro Devices sunk more than 14%.

Democrats who flipped critical races on Tuesday also singled out big tech companies and data centers.

South Carolina Democrat Annie Andrews, who flipped a Senate seat in the state on Tuesday, launched a website during the campaign that branded her opponent — Sen. Darline Graham — "data center Darline."

In her victory speech on Tuesday, Andrews said voters chose a South Carolina "where decisions about communities are made by the people who live in our communities, not the billionaires or data center companies in Washington D.C."

Ohio Democrat Sherrod Brown, who is heading back to the Senate after defeating incumbent Republican Sen. Jon Husted on Tuesday, said in his victory speech, "I do not work for data centers."

Democratic governors also attacked data centers in their victory speeches. Pennsylvania Gov. Josh Shapiro, who signed an executive order in August to slow data center development, said big tech companies shouldn't determine residents' futures. "Instead, the folks in our local communities get the final say," he said.

Perhaps most important to the data center build-out is Texas, which is home to 27% of planned data center capacity, according to Wells Fargo.

Gina Hinojosa, the Democrat who ousted incumbent Republican Gov. Greg Abbott, didn't address data centers directly in her victory speech, though they played a key role in her campaign.

"When I am governor, I will issue a moratorium, a ban, on all data center approvals until laws can be passed by the legislature to protect Texans," she said in a video posted to her X in late October. "This is about one fight. Is this going to be a Texas that is by and for the people, or is this the billionaire's world and we just live here? Data centers are part of that fight."

#2863. LeBron Log No. 3: The King dishes, Caitlin Clark visits and closing time

LeBron Log No. 3: The King dishes, Caitlin Clark visits and closing time originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

LeBron James' latest week with the Sixers included his first 10-assist game with Philadelphia, his best performance with the team, a visit with Caitlin Clark, a crowd-wide finger wag and one jumper he would probably like back.

Some of it belonged in the box score. The rest quickly made its way around social media.

Welcome to the third edition of the LeBron Log.

Throughout the preseason, the Sixers discussed the responsibility that accompanied their desire to play faster.

They wanted to advance the ball before defenses could organize, but Nick Nurse was not interested in accepting extra turnovers as part of the tradeoff. When asked whether he could live with more mistakes if they came with better pace, his answer was simple.

Seven games into the regular season, the Sixers have not yet become one of the NBA's fastest teams. They entered Monday ranked 20th in pace.

Philadelphia has committed an NBA-low 12.7 turnovers per game. James has helped organize the offense as its leading facilitator, recording 35 assists against 11 turnovers.

He leads the Sixers in total assists despite playing only five games. Tyrese Maxey, who has appeared in all seven, is second with 31.

James provided the cleanest example Wednesday night against the Pacers. He finished with 13 points, 10 assists, five rebounds, two blocks and no turnovers during the Sixers' 114-109 comeback victory.

It was his first double-digit-assist performance with Philadelphia. According to StatMuse, James also became the oldest player in its database to record at least 10 points and 10 assists while committing no more than one turnover.

James' night was not flashy by his standards. He attempted 12 shots and finished fifth on the team in scoring. Still, he helped five Sixers reach double figures without surrendering a possession.

The Sixers signed the NBA's all-time leading scorer. Through the opening weeks of his Philadelphia career, he has been their leading passer.

James entered his first NBA Cup game with the Sixers possessing more tournament history than anyone else on the roster.

He led the Lakers to the inaugural Cup championship in 2023 and was named the event's first MVP after averaging 26.4 points, eight rebounds and seven assists during Los Angeles' unbeaten run.

Three years later, his first Cup game in red, white and blue produced his best Sixers performance yet.

James recorded 20 points, nine rebounds, eight assists, one steal and one block in Friday's 98-93 win over the Knicks. He shot 8 for 15 and saved two of his most important baskets for the final 70 seconds.

Jalen Brunson's three-pointer gave New York a 90-89 lead with 1:32 remaining. On the ensuing possession, James backed down OG Anunoby, turned toward the middle and sank a fadeaway to move the Sixers back in front.

VJ Edgecombe extended the lead with a three-pointer as the shot clock expired. After Brunson made two free throws, Maxey drove for a layup and missed. James followed the play, collected the offensive rebound and scored the putback with 11.9 seconds remaining.

The basket sealed Philadelphia's second win over the defending champions and its first of NBA Cup group play.

"Best game all around," Nurse said. "He's been good, but that was his best game for sure."

James has played more than 1,600 regular-season games, but his Philadelphia closing reel has officially begun.

The fourth-quarter baskets were not James' only memorable moments Friday.

After converting an and-one earlier in the game, James broke out his familiar shoulder-shrug celebration. Edgecombe joined him, smiling as he mirrored the move alongside his 41-year-old teammate.

The pair had already developed a personalized handshake featuring two taps to the head and a forceful shrug. This one came during the game.

James supplied another recognizable celebration in the third quarter.

Anunoby drove toward the basket with James trailing the play. James caught him near the rim and was officially credited with his fifth block of the season.

He turned toward the crowd and wagged his finger. The fans joined in.

Edgecombe later delivered the biggest shot of the game, and James met him with an embrace near the sideline. The two are separated by more than 20 years, but they have quickly developed a collection of celebrations.

"He doesn't shy away from the moment," James said of the 21-year-old. "Young kid, but he thrives in the moment."

James was not the only basketball attraction inside Gainbridge Fieldhouse on Wednesday.

Indiana Fever star Caitlin Clark watched the Sixers' win from courtside and brought a small camera with her. It was the second time this year she photographed James during a game.

In March, Clark served as a credentialed photographer when James and the Lakers visited Indiana.

"The KING 👑 … Shot by ME," Clark wrote when she shared those photos.

After Wednesday's game, Clark made her way toward the visiting locker room. She briefly met Maxey before speaking with James in the hallway.

James gave Clark one of his No. 23 Sixers jerseys. The team shared photos of the exchange with a fitting caption.

Clark publicly expressed hope that James would join the Pacers before he signed with Philadelphia. Their mutual respect goes back further.

During Clark's first WNBA season in 2024, James compared the attention surrounding her professional debut to what he experienced when he entered the NBA directly from high school.

"I'm rooting for Caitlin because I've been in that seat before," James said at the time. "I've walked that road before."

James did not speak with reporters after Wednesday's game, so he was not asked about their conversation.

Maxey was asked about meeting Clark for the first time.

"That's just another person that I like to watch," he said. "She cold. She nice. Seeing her today was really cool. Sometimes you try not to fan out, but it was really cool seeing her. It's her building; she plays here too."

James and Edgecombe also produced the week's most widely shared Sixers warmup clip.

Before Wednesday's game, the two danced together near the three-point line. Edgecombe moved away while James stepped back and launched a jumper.

The clip spread quickly across social media. James still walked out of downtown Indianapolis with 10 assists, no turnovers and a victory.

James has made more field goals than anyone in NBA history. Even the career scoring leader is entitled to one warmup mulligan.

#2862. NASCAR Championship Weekend Gets Important Weather Update Ahead of Homestead Finale

NASCAR Championship Weekend Gets Important Weather Update Ahead of Homestead Finale

NASCAR's championship weekend is finally here, and with three national series titles on the line at Homestead-Miami Speedway, the last thing anyone wants is a weather delay.

Fortunately, the early forecast offers encouraging news for Sunday's Cup Series championship race. There is some rain in the outlook for the weekend, but the latest projections suggest NASCAR could get through its biggest race of the season without a major interruption.

Temperatures, however, will be another story.

South Florida is expected to remain hot throughout the three-day championship weekend, with daytime highs approaching 90 degrees. That could make for demanding conditions inside the race cars, particularly during Sunday's season finale.

Here's what fans and teams can expect as NASCAR prepares to crown its 2026 champions.

Friday, Nov. 6, brings the first championship race of the weekend, with the Craftsman Truck Series scheduled to take the green flag at 7:30 p.m. ET.

The forecast calls for a high of 88 degrees, with a mix of clouds and sunshine and a slight chance of a rain shower. North-northeast winds are expected at 5 to 10 mph.

By Friday evening, conditions should improve, with mostly clear skies, a few passing clouds and temperatures eventually falling toward 70 degrees overnight.

Saturday's outlook is similar. The high is expected to reach 88 degrees, with intervals of sunshine and clouds and another slight chance of a passing shower. Winds should remain light and variable.

The NASCAR O'Reilly Auto Parts Series championship race is scheduled for 5 p.m. ET Saturday, meaning drivers could face considerable heat before temperatures begin to ease later in the evening. Saturday night's forecast calls for occasional clouds and a low of 72 degrees.

The most important forecast of the weekend belongs to Sunday, Nov. 8, when NASCAR will crown its Cup Series champion.

As of Thursday, the outlook is promising.

Sunday's forecast calls for partly cloudy skies and a high of 87 degrees, with north-northeast winds at 5 to 10 mph. Unlike Friday and Saturday, the daily forecast does not specifically mention a chance of rain.

That's welcome news with the Cup Series championship race scheduled to begin at 3 p.m. ET on NBC.

The heat could still play a role. With temperatures approaching the upper 80s, managing conditions inside the cockpit will be another consideration for drivers competing for the title.

For fans planning to attend, sunscreen, hydration and preparation for prolonged exposure to the sun will be important throughout the weekend.

Weather forecasts can change, particularly in South Florida, and even a brief shower could affect NASCAR's on-track schedule. But with three championships to decide and the Cup Series season coming down to one final race, the current outlook gives teams and fans reason for optimism.

#2858. The midterm election could reshape the muni bond market. Here’s what investors need to know

The outcome of Tuesday's election is expected to have broad implications across the market, including municipal bonds.

The spotlight has been on the battle for congressional control and whether Democrats will wrest the House of Representatives, the Senate or both from Republicans. But for the muni market, what happens at the state level could have bigger repercussions — at least in the near term.

There is an array of initiatives on ballots around the nation that could change tax laws and add to bond supply. Taxes matter to muni investors since the income received is free of federal tax, as well as state tax if the holder lives in the state where the bond is issued.

Depending on the location, ballot measures are up for approval that would either provide tax breaks, or raise taxes on the wealthy.

"It's a complete bifurcation between red and blue states," said Dan Close, head of municipals at Nuveen. "Blue states are trying to put in place graduated income tax structures, trying to capture more revenue from high-income earners. Red states are promoting affordability by trying to cut property taxes."

Red states are those that predominantly vote Republican, while blue states swing toward Democrats.

For instance, California has a proposition known as the "billionaire tax," which would impose a one-time levy equal to 5% of the wealth of certain taxpayers with at least $1 billion in assets. The state also has a measure on the ballot that would make a temporary tax hike permanent on individuals earning over $371,000, adjusted annually for inflation. The tax is currently set to expire in 2031.

"The impact to the muni bond market will be to make the exemption more valuable," Close said of the possible passage of tax increases. Investors will likely seek shelter in the muni market, which would send bond prices higher, he noted.

It is a different story in Florida, where voters are set to decide whether to increase the Sunshine State's homestead exemption for non-school district property taxes. Qualified homeowners currently have a $25,000 property tax exemption, as well as another for $25,000 that doesn't apply to school property taxes. The new proposal raises the latter exemption to $150,000 in 2027 and $250,000 by 2028. New residents have a five-year waiting period before receiving the benefit.

If passed, some local governments will have to find other revenue sources or increase the overall tax rate to replace what they've lost, said Tom Kozlik, head of public policy and municipal strategy at HilltopSecurities.

"It's not necessarily going to result in downgrades for sure, but there would have to be some balancing with regard to overall revenues," he said.

Not all municipalities will be hurt equally, which means credit research is important, said Close.

"Like everything in municipal finance, it is really on a case-by-case basis that you could have some credit deterioration from [a] rollback of property taxes," he said.

Other voters on Tuesday will be asked to approve more state or local government debt. That could weigh on a market that has already seen heightened supply.

Some $473.8 billion has already been issued year to date, through September, according to the Securities Industry and Financial Markets Association. That's a 7.8% increase over the same period a year ago. In 2025, total muni issuance reached $587.3 billion, 14.3% higher than 2024, Sifma said.

Cooper Howard, director of fixed income research and strategy at the Schwab Center for Financial Research, believes supply is the biggest issue coming out of the election.

"That amount of issuance has been one of the reasons why you have to see that strong amount of demand continue to keep pace with it for total returns to keep up," Howard said.

Fortunately, new supply doesn't necessarily come quickly into the market. "There's a window over a couple years that they can issue," he noted.

The ultimate makeup of the next Congress could also have consequences for muni investors as lawmakers sort out their priorities. Most notable is what lawmakers will do to tackle the country's affordability problem, Kozlik at Hilltop said.

"There could be some tax policy initiatives and even affordability-related initiatives that manifest themselves in the first quarter of next year," he said. "That could impact housing. It could impact higher education. It could impact public power financing."

Investors considering moves before the election may want to look at those states with tax increases on the ballot, said Close at Nuveen.

For instance, Nuveen recently began offering a separately managed account dedicated to Washington after getting an enormous number of inquiries from financial advisors. The state recently enacted a 9.9% tax on individuals with taxable annual income above $1 million, set to go into effect in 2028. But the issue is now being put to voters, who can decide whether to repeal the new law.

"At the margin, if you do think that some of these measures are going to pass, knowing that bonds usually move up with an increase in taxes, there might be some opportunities to buy ahead of the election for blue states," Close said.

Howard believes munis are attractive right now, particularly for those who are in a tax bracket of 24% or above. Fundamentals are good and the bonds largely have good credit quality, he said. "About 70% of the broad muni index is either triple-A or double-A rated."

Kozlik is also bullish, calling yields "really attractive." He suggests investors concentrate on AA-rated or higher bonds.

"The muni space is part of the portfolio that most investors want," he said. "It helps them sleep well at night."

#2855. 3 takeaways from Sixers’ win over Pistons: This feels like a special group

The Sixers avenged their only loss of the young season with a 112-106 win over the Pistons in Detroit Wednesday.

It was essentially a wire-to-wire win, though it did get a bit dicey at the end. Still, the Sixers' resiliency in closing out close games remains impressive. They now have the best record in the NBA at 8-1.

Though Jalen Duren remained out for the Pistons, it was another good win for the Sixers, who remain undefeated on the road. Here are some takeaways from the victory.

Jaylen Brown was a two-way monster in this one. He was on the attack early, dropping 10 points in the first quarter and 13 in the second. He scored at all three levels, going for a coast-to-coast dunk finish over Paul Reed, operating plenty in his office in the midrange and nailing a deep three at the buzzer to close the first. He was 11-of-19 from the field and 3-of-5 from deep, finishing with a game-high 30 points.

Though VJ Edgecombe was the primary defender most of the night, Cade Cunningham saw plenty of Brown as well. They held the All-Star guard to 26 points on 9-of-22 from the field. Not a perfect night, but Cunningham had to work for everything he got.

Brown was great all night, but his biggest contribution came when it mattered most. With 38 seconds left and the Sixers clinging to a 106-102 lead, Cunningham saw an opportunity in transition to fire a triple and make it a one-point game. It was a clean look … until it wasn't. Brown recovered and came away with a massive block. From there, the Sixers put the game away at the free throw line.

Sure, there are advanced analytics that can measure a guy making "winning plays," but it's a difficult thing to quantify. It just shows up on the stat sheet as a block, but it was a game-sealing play — the kind you need to see on the court, not a spreadsheet.

As of this posting, the Sixers are second in the NBA in defensive rating. While I do think the national narrative about their defense heading into the season was slightly overblown, I wasn't expecting it to be elite right out of the gate.

The obvious locks were Brown and Edgecombe. Brown has proven to be an elite on-ball defender, while Edgecombe has all the makings of one. LeBron James has seen slippage in recent years on that end, but has been mostly excellent this season. Tyrese Maxey's defensive growth has continued as he's developed from a liability to a legitimately impactful defender.

But the biggest reason the Sixers' defense has flourished is Joel Embiid. He's moving better than he has in over two years. Though he's not quite at the level of rim protection he was during his All-Defensive Team days, he's affecting more shots at the rim. He's also looked comfortable on the perimeter — hedging, blitzing and even switching onto quicker players.

And we all know rebounding has been a massive issue here in recent seasons. Under Nick Nurse, the Sixers have finished in the bottom five in defensive rebounding percentage the last three years. As of now, the Sixers are ninth in the NBA in defensive rebounding percentage (I know, I had to double-check that one myself). Ending possessions has been huge for their defense. Embiid pulled down 15 boards Wednesday. The last time he pulled down 15 or more rebounds in regulation of a regular-season game was Jan. 22, 2024, the night he dropped 70 on Victor Wembanyama and the San Antonio Spurs.

Will this keep up? A lot of that is going to depend on health, but if they continue to play defense like this, just wait until the offense catches up.

You just get a feeling about teams early on sometimes. This group at 8-1 — playing defense, making hustle plays, closing out close games, getting along and having fun — feels special. I know, most teams that LeBron plays on feel special, but there's this focus and drive that I've never seen in any Sixers team I've covered.

They're going to hit adversity. An injury or two is sure to pop up. The offense is still in the bottom third of the league. The backup center situation is murky at best.

But man, this feels like a team worth getting excited about.

#2853. Ben Johnson was impressed with the Buccaneers' all-run offense

Bears coach Ben Johnson is well known as an offensive innovator and creative play caller. But he was also impressed that the team he’s preparing to coach against this week just won a game with as old-school an offense as the modern NFL has ever seen.

The Bears will face the Buccaneers on Sunday night, and Johnson has been watching film of the way the Bucs beat the Falcons last week while completing zero passes, the first time in more than half a century that an NFL team finished a game without a completion. Johnson said Bucs coach Todd Bowles and his staff wisely kept the ball on the ground on a rainy day in Tampa, and Johnson said the Bucs have great personnel for such an approach.

“That’s a very unique game, and credit to Coach Bowles for recognizing early on, ‘We’re not even going to throw the ball, we’re going to run it.’ And they’ve got the horses to do that,” Johnson said. “This offensive line is really good. The scheme is very good — they’ve got a lot of variety with the way they do things. And their backs are phenomenal as well.”

The Buccaneers and the Bears rank first and second in the NFL in rushing, with the Bucs averaging 168.6 yards per game and the Bears averaging 150.8. Johnson has been particularly impressed with the way the Bucs have run the ball over the last four weeks, with Baker Mayfield missing three of those games and not completing any passes in the fourth.

“It’s a potent rushing attack,” Johnson said. “I think the last four games they’re averaging 230 a game, and it coincides with them winning three out of their last four as well, so that’ll be a huge challenge for us. They’re the No. 1 team in football rushing the ball right now. It’ll be a big one.”

Against the Falcons, the Buccaneers finished with 326 more rushing yards than passing yards. It’s safe to say they won’t have that great a run-pass disparity on Sunday night against the Bears, but Johnson knows the Buccaneers’ offense can be dangerous even if it’s one-dimensional.

#2849. Dianna Russini comeback timeline: What we know from Mike Vrabel controversy to NFL media return

Dianna Russini comeback timeline: What we know from Mike Vrabel controversy to NFL media return originally appeared on The Sporting News. Add The Sporting News as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

It was a whirlwind of an offseason for Patriots coach Mike Vrabel, who was linked romantically to longtime NFL reporter Dianna Russini after a series of photos showed the two spending time together over a period of six years.

Vrabel, who is married, saw his ordeal play out fairly publicly. He addressed reporters multiple times as the scandal unfolded before the NFL Draft, and he announced in April that he would be going to counseling with his family.

For Russini, who is also married, the saga has largely played out behind closed doors. Russini released her resignation letter from The Athletic in April, but she hasn't spoken on camera since. That could be about to change.

Reports have indicated Russini is planning a comeback, at which point she might tell her side of the story.

Here's a look back at the timeline leading to Russini's comeback.

MORE:Full timeline of Vrabel-Russini scandal

In a statement to NBC News on Wednesday, Athletic Executive Editor Steven Ginsberg said the photos are "misleading and lack essential context."

"These were public interactions in front of many people," Ginsberg said. "Dianna is a premier journalist covering the NFL and we're proud to have her at The Athletic."

Russini said in her own statement that many reporters in her field interact with "sources" outside of stadiums and work venues.

"The photos don't represent the group of six people who were hanging out during the day," she said.

Russini released a lengthy letter announcing her resignation from The Athletic on April 14, a week after the photos were first published.

The longtime reporter did not admit to a relationship with Vrabel or walk back her initial defense, instead explaining that her contract was already set to expire and adding that she did not want the growing story to "define" her career.

Russini referenced a "narrative that has been constructed" about her and Vrabel, continuing to insist that their interaction in Arizona was coincidental and innocent. Vrabel, meanwhile, would later say that he had to have "difficult conversations" with his family and said he needed to focus on being "the best husband, father and coach that I can possibly be."

The Athletic finished its internal review of Russini in September, nearly five months after she resigned, finding that her relationship with Vrabel was in "clear violation" of the outlet's standards.

"Regardless of whether the relationship was romantic, it was a breach and should have been disclosed," the outlet said in its findings.

The New York Post, which broke the original story through Page Six, reported that Russini plans to tell her side of the story upon accepting her next job.

Russini "continues to mull offers" from different outlets, the report claimed, though her return did not turn out to be imminent.

The report was one of the first indications that Russini was planning to dive back into the NFL media landscape, though it didn't make her destination any clearer.

While Russini didn't accept a job as quickly as September's report might have indicated, Page Six reported on Nov. 5 that the embattled reporter is on the verge of a return to the media world.

Russini will be "headlining her own show," the report said, adding that it will be launching within the next week. NBC Sports first reported that Russini's return was imminent.

"It's been in the works for a while and she can't wait to get back to what she loves," a source told Page Six.

What no report indicated is the ultimate destination for Russini. While she could launch her own show independently, the September report that she was mulling offers from outlets indicates it will be on an established platform.

#2848. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on these 3 stocks for the long haul

The ongoing earnings season, Middle East tensions and buzz ahead of the U.S. midterm elections have been keeping investors busy. Further, worries about elevated artificial intelligence spending are weighing on investor sentiment.

Investors looking beyond the short-term noise and focusing on long-term returns can track top Wall Street analysts to pick the right stocks, backed by strong research.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

This week's first stock pick is Datadog, an AI-powered observability and security platform. The company will announce its results for the third quarter on Nov. 4. Datadog is seeing strong demand for its offerings, thanks to cloud migration and rapid AI adoption. TipRanks' KPIs tool for DDOG shows the growth in its customer base over the past few years amid increasing market penetration and growing adoption of its products.

Heading into Q3 FY26 earnings, Bank of America analyst Koji Ikeda reiterated a buy rating on Datadog stock and raised his price target to $335 from $305. The analyst noted that industry checks and data indicate that infrastructure software demand is strengthening, with AI increasingly driving usage and growth.

Ikeda called Datadog and Atlassian his top picks, saying that companies that can show that AI is a tailwind to growth could see their valuation rerate, even at premium multiples. He added that the total addressable market for observability is growing faster than anticipated, driven by accelerated growth of enterprise AI and the rapid ramp of consumer AI agents.

The five-star analyst believes that focus will be on revenue growth, which he estimates to be 28.7% for Q3 FY26 versus the Street's consensus of 29.1%. Ikeda thinks that the market is underestimating the growth prospects of DDOG's security products. Meanwhile, he cautioned investors about concentration risk related to large customers.

"We think results will be supportive of our long-term thesis that Datadog is a strong share gainer in the rapidly expanding observability opportunity," said Ikeda.

Ikeda ranks No. 673 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 55% of the time, delivering an average return of 10.9%. See Datadog Statistics on TipRanks.

The next pick, semiconductor giant Nvidia, is also benefiting from AI-driven demand for its products. After attending Nvidia's GTC Berlin conference, Morgan Stanley analyst Joseph Moore reaffirmed a buy rating on NVDA stock with a price target of $300, calling it a top pick in the semiconductor space.

The five-star analyst said that the conference supported his thesis that Nvidia continues to effectively navigate bottlenecks in the supply chain, land/power/shell, and funding. In fact, Moore is "convinced that all 3 aspects are opportunities for NVDA."

While Nvidia's GTC Berlin offered no major updates, Moore noted the conference reflected that the company continues to add to its significant advantages heading into 2027. The analyst said that Vera Rubin is gaining momentum, though details on next year's Rubin Ultra were lighter than expected.

With demand outpacing supply, Moore said that focus is on closing the gap. In this regard, he highlighted the supply chain deals on substrate, packaging and testing. While the analyst continues to view TSMC 3 nm wafer supply as the biggest challenge, he expects it to ease over time.

Moore ranks No. 142 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 59% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.4%. See Nvidia Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Aerospace company SpaceX recently announced solid third-quarter results, with its AI revenue surging to $7.56 billion from $813 million in the prior-year quarter.

Impressed by the results, Needham analyst Ryan Koontz reiterated a buy rating on SPCX stock with a price target of $250. Koontz highlighted that SpaceX's Q3 results significantly exceeded expectations, proving that the company's vertically integrated business model is driving massive scale.

Furthermore, Koontz emphasized the 195.2% sequential growth in SpaceX's AI revenue, fueled by wholesale spot compute deals. He noted management's increasing focus on its AI strategy, given that it is optimistic about adding 10 gigawatts of incremental capacity during 2027.

"With increasing confidence on Starship re-use, we meaningfully increase our estimates," said Koontz.

Koontz ranks No. 357 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 42% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.7%. See SpaceX Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

#2847. Jared Verse was upset that Browns teammates didn't react when he took a cheap shot

Browns defensive end Jared Verse was picked up and thrown to the ground by Steelers guard Mason McCormick on Sunday, and McCormick wasn’t the only person Verse was upset with afterward.

Verse told reporters in Cleveland today that he was upset both by McCormick’s cheap shot and by his teammates’ reaction, or lack thereof. Verse thought the rest of the Browns’ players should have had his back, and didn’t.

“To be quite honest with you, that was the play that made me really angry,” Verse said. “I was already upset about the game, the way things were moving, but at that point it became deep. You’re doing dirty shit, you’re trying to sweep my legs out. That honestly could have put me in a position to not be able to finish the season, put me in a position to change my life in a very bad aspect, so that upset me. The lack of reaction by those around me had upset me a lot. They were focused on the game and everything like that, but a similar play happened to Derek Barnett on the next field goal, and I spazzed, I lost it. Because those are my brothers, those are my teammates, and when you do something like that to them, it upsets me deeply. We have to get better, we have to be aware. These are my teammates, these are my brothers, and we’re not fighting for each other.”

Verse said he addressed his feelings with his teammates. Verse did acknowledge that he brought some of it on himself by the trash talk he was directing at the Steelers, but he still felt that his teammates should have protected him when it escalated from verbal to physical.

“That’s a conversation that we’ve had, we’ve spoken about,” Verse said. “I made my concerns known, I’ve made my issues known about the situation, but also, I didn’t put them in a position to let them know that, I didn’t let them know beforehand I was going to talk to the Steelers and Steeler Nation. I’ve got to be better.”

Verse, the 2024 NFL Defensive Rookie of the Year who arrived in Cleveland in the trade that sent Myles Garrett to the Rams, has been a disappointment this season. His teammates may be wishing he’d focus a little less on talking trash and getting into altercations on the field, and a little more on sacking the opposing quarterback — something he hasn’t done yet this season.

#2845. Prominent Spine Physician Shares Encouraging Outlook on Lions LB Jack Campbell

The Detroit Lions have no choice but to press on in the wake of the frightening injury to linebacker Jack Campbell, who was hospitalized after taking a heavy hit during Sunday's victory over the divisional rival Minnesota Vikings.

The Lions, who also lost linebacker Jimmy Rolder for the season, will be taking on the Miami Dolphins this Sunday and looking to improve their record to 6-2.

The good news for the Lions and Campbell in particular is that the linebacker released a statement in which he reported full movement in his lower body, and that movement in his upper body is progressing.

And now, a prominent spine surgeon watching from afar has offered an encouraging perspective.

According to Dr. Ari Berg, a noted Spine Surgeon at Hackensack Meridian Health, Campbell's update is an encouraging sign.

"At the very center of the spinal cord is where you have control of the arms," Berg said via The New York Post. "More toward the periphery of the spinal cord, you have control of the legs and that's why when you have this contusion of the center of the spinal cord, it's more common that you see this weakness in the arms and the upper extremities more so than the legs."

"[The] long-term prognosis is still, we still need a little bit more information before we can make that judgment," he continued. "It could be weeks. It could be months. It could take longer than that. It's just too soon to call….Again, encouraging that he's already shown improvements and hopefully he continues on that upper trajectory."

In a special statement released through the official Lions' X account, Campbell gave the latest update on his condition while also thanking the organization and fans for their prayers and well wishes.

"I'm incredibly encouraged by the progress I've made in such a short period of time. I have full movement in my lower body, and I'm continuing to make progress with movement in my upper body," Campbell's statement read. "I know there's still work ahead, but I remain very optimistic."

I'm taking things one day at a time, staying positive, and focusing on getting better. My faith in God has never been stronger. I know He has a plan for me, and I trust in that plan. I know there may be challenges ahead, but with my faith, my family, and so many people in my corner, I know we'll get through this. I especially want to thank the athletic trainers and medical personnel who responded and cared for me on the field. I know those were difficult moments, and I'm incredibly grateful for the professionalism, compassion, and care you showed me."

"One thing I've learned about Detroit is that this city is built on toughness, resilience, and grit. That's exactly how I'm going to approach this challenge. Please continue to keep me and my family in your prayers. We feel the love, and I can't thank everyone enough."

#2844. Kyler Murray out for Monday vs. Bills, week-to-week with hamstring injury

Kyler Murray’s hamstring injury is serious enough that it will sideline him for at least a week.

Vikings head coach Kevin O’Connell told reporters in his Thursday press conference that Murray is out for Monday night’s game against the Bills with a hamstring strain and is considered week-to-week.

Carson Wentz will start Week 9, which will be his second start of the season.

“For [Murray], his movement skills, and the way he plays the game, we do feel like we’d be putting him at risk of possibly turning this into a more significant injury than it is by trying to have him work through it here early on,” O’Connell said. “So, Carson will start the football game and Max [Brosmer] will back him up.”

While it didn’t seem like O’Connell was too concerned about Murray’s availability earlier in the week, further testing revealed the extent of the issue.

“I think hamstrings are always unique,” O’Connell said. "[Y]ou always have to understand that whatever that picture or that imaging says, sometimes it’s an injury you can really hone in on. That’s why I wanted to be certain before I went speculating on Monday just because I wanted to be sure it wasn’t something that would prohibit him from playing — until it was.

“I’m not going to get into the details of the grade or the injury or the level medically speaking, but just knowing that Kyler needs his mobility — it’s a big, huge part of what makes him an effective player. If that puts him at risk of potentially adding to the grade of the injury, we can’t do that. It’s a long season. We hope to get him back and get him going.”

O’Connell noted injured reserve is not a consideration for Murray with this injury.

Wentz stepped in and played after Murray suffered a concussion earlier this season. Wentz completed 23-of-39 passes against the Packers and Bears for 276 yards with three touchdowns and no interceptions.

“Obviously, there’s a ton of confidence in Carson,” O’Connell said. “He’s had a major, major role in two wins this year against two really good teams. He’s a steadying presence and a guy we know can go in and play good football, that’s why we feel so great about having him here.

“He’s going to throw a lot of completions. He’s going to put the ball in play. He’s got such a high, high understanding of not only the offense but what he’s looking at on the other side. He has 100 games of experience. He’s the perfect guy in a moment where you can’t have Kyler out there at full strength to go in there. And we feel very strongly about when Carson’s in there, we’re going to get really effective quarterback play.”

Minnesota’s offense has struggled to generate yards and points this season, with the club ranking No. 27 in scoring and No. 29 in total yards. We’ll see if Wentz can provide a spark to change that on Monday night.

#2843. An investigation regarding the Jalen Hurts situation has not begun

The return of Eagles quarterback Jalen Hurts to the team’s facility on Wednesday is a reminder that plenty of questions remain regarding the decisions made as to his health before flying to London with a blood clot in his leg, playing in London with a blood clot in his leg, and flying back from London with a blood clot in his leg.

A widespread presumption has emerged that the NFL and NFL Players Association have commenced a joint investigation into the care and recommendations Hurts received from team and league doctors. However, the investigation has yet to start.

The statement issued by the league and the NFLPA said only that they are “working together to review the matter.” A formal investigation has not yet begun, of any kind. The union has the right under the Collective Bargaining Agreement to initiate an investigation. That hasn’t happened, either.

If the NFLPA will be working with the NFL in any official investigation, it’s critical that the union insist on a process that will be fair, objective, and transparent. It can’t be a CYA exercise.

Basically, it can’t give off the same vibes that have emanated from the team and the league, to date. The union can’t afford to create the impression that it’s complicit in a coverup. And the process needs to lead to real change, aimed at ensuring that someone disconnected from the team (and possibly from the league, especially as to international games) will be involved in medical decisions as to potentially serious health situations.

The NFL and the NFLPA already employ an unaffiliated doctor when it comes to diagnosing concussions and clearing players to return after suffering concussions. Other health conditions require a similar layer of detachment, in order to ensure that the shared incentives of the team and the player for the player to play will not override the importance of treating the player like a patient.

The Hornets (3-6) attributed Knueppel's exit to "soreness in his left leg" and head coach Charles Lee did not have an update after the game.

He finished with three points, all coming on free throws, and two turnovers.

If Knueppel is going to miss more time, it's a tough blow for a Hornets team that was one of the biggest surprises last season, going 44-38 a year after finishing 19-63.

Charlotte selected Knueppel No. 4 overall out of Duke in the 2025 NBA Draft, and he finished his rookie season with averages of 18.5 points, 5.3 rebounds and 3.4 assists per game while shooting 47.5 percent from the field, including an impressive 42.5 percent from 3-point range on 7.9 attempts per game.

The Hornets decided to shift course despite their strong 2025-26 campaign, sending LaMelo Ball to the Timberwolves as part of a four-team trade that saw Charlotte acquire big man Naz Reid (a former NBA Sixth Man of the Year), along with an unprotected 2033 first-round pick, three first-round pick swaps and three second-round picks.

Brooklyn Nets forward Michael Porter Jr. defends Charlotte Hornets guard Kon Knueppel during a game on Wednesday, Nov. 4, 2026, in Brooklyn, N.Y. Corey Sipkin for the NY POST

Reid has been off to a strong start, averaging 16.9 points and 8.3 rebounds per game, but the Hornets have hit a five-game skid after starting the season 3-1.

Brandon Miller, the No. 2 overall pick out of Alabama in 2023, is leading the Hornets in scoring at 22 points per game after averaging 20.2 points in 65 games last season.

But without Knueppel, the Hornets will have a hard time snapping their decade-long playoff drought.

#2841. Chiefs WR Rashee Rice files for bankruptcy, claiming more than $3.45 million in debt

Kansas City Chiefs wide receiver Rashee Rice filed for Chapter 7 bankruptcy on Wednesday, Nov. 4, claiming millions in liabilities, much of which was tied to a 2024 car crash.

Rice claimed just $677,054 in personal assets against more than $3.45 million in debts, according to the filing. More than $3.27 million was the product of unsecured debts via civil litigation, business debt and consumer debt.

Among Rice's listed assets in the filing were a 2023 Mercedes-Benz valued at $53,800 and a Super Bowl 58 ring valued at $160,000.

In March 2024, Rice was driving 119 mph in a Lamborghini Urus on a Dallas highway before causing a six-car crash, according to an arrest warrant. The following July, he pleaded guilty to third-degree felony charges of collision involving serious bodily injury and racing on a highway causing bodily injury.

Rice was found to have violated his probation in May 2026 after testing positive for marijuana. He was ordered to spend 30 days in jail.

Attorney Marc Lenahan told WFAA in July 2025 his client, Kathryn Kuykendall, had not received payment after Rice agreed to a $1 million settlement when an Uber she was riding in was struck by Rice's car.

Of the 20 creditors listed, Kuykendall has the largest outstanding claim at more than $979,000.

Rice, 26, is in the final year of a four-year, $6.5 million contract he signed as a second-round pick out of Southern Methodist in 2023. His base salary for 2026 was $1.635 million, with a prorated bonus of nearly $431,000.
